About the Authors

Dr. Joel D. Wallach, BS, DVM, ND

 

Dr. Wallach has been involved in biomedical research
and clinical medicine for more than 50 years. He received
his B.S. degree (agriculture) from the University of Missouri
with a major in animal husbandry (nutrition) and a minor
in field crops and soils; D.V.M. (doctorate in veterinary
medicine) from the University of Missouri; three year post
doctoral fellowship (comparative pathology/ medicine) from
Washington University, St. Louis, MO - The Center for the
Biology of Natural Systems (the St. Louis Zoological Gardens,
Shaw’s Botanical Gardens, Botany Department, Washington
University); Iowa State University (Department of Pathology
and the State Diagnostic Laboratory), Ames, IA; Natal
Parks Board, Umfolozi and Hluhluwe Game Parks, Natal,
Republic of South Africa (Operation Rhino: Conservation of
the Southern White Rhino - project veterinarian and capture
officer); Southern Rhodesia (Zimbabwe), Wanke Game
Park (Operation Elephant - consulting capture officer);St.
Louis Zoological Gardens; The Chicago Zoological Gardens
(Brookfield Zoo); Shedd Aquarium, Chicago, IL; Yerkes
Regional Primate Research Center, Emory University, Atlanta,
GA; N.D. (naturopathic physician)from the National College
of Naturopathic Medicine, Portland, OR; Harbin Medical
University, Harbin, Hei Long Jiang and the Shanghai Medical
University, Peoples Republic of China.

He was a member of NIH site visit teams for facilities
using exotic animal models for the study of human diseases
for four years and was a member of the 1968 National

Immortality Vv

 

Introduction

In the great scheme of things, the quest for a long
and healthful life is a relatively new goal for humans. In the
beginning, humans didn’t know how long they could live,
there were no bench marks or records or living examples -
getting food, satisfying a salt hunger and survival itself were
the universal drives of life.

We do know now, without a doubt, that humans can
and do live well beyond 100 years of age - centenarians - the
old ones; and super-centenarians older than 110 years are not
that uncommon. ;

While in fact, we all have the genetic equipment to live
healthfully well beyond 100 - consciously getting there today
for the majority, however, requires a profound commitment
to a time proven system, and a daily call to focused action
- becoming a centenarian is a matter of proven, universal,
proactive, yet simple choices and actions.

Most centenarians in the “Blue Zones” throughout
the world get there without any conscious effort - in effect
they get into the centenarian club by simply “falling off the
turnip truck.” They become centenarians because of where
they live and because of successful cultural practices that
have been developed and perfected by trial and error over
the millennium.

When centenarians or super-centenarians are asked for
their sage wisdom on how they got to be 100 years of age, it is
quite obvious they themselves don’t know - they will answer
the ultimate, scientific, deep and thoughtful question with a
quaint response such as, “have a good sense of humor,” or

s

===== Screenshot (10) =====
vi Immortality

“think good thoughts every day,” or “love everyone,” or
“keep moving.”

Becoming a centenarian is not related to “having a good
sense of humor” or to an accident of “good genes” - we now
know, reaching 100 years of age is a by-product of something
else - a collection of four simple, universal, repeatable, daily
habits and endeavors, many of which on the surface, are
seemingly meaningless and unrelated to each other, our
health and longevity, and yet they hold the unforgiving key
to IMMORTALITY.

Unfortunately, we Americans and people of other
industrialized nations, with an almost religious fervor,
believe that their doctors, “break through” pharmaceuticals
and comprehensive health plans will get us healthfully into
the centenarian club - yet, despite spending three quarters
of the world’s annual expenditure for health care, according
to the CDC and the WHO, we Americans ranked a dismal
48th in the world for health and longevity in 2008 - hardly
a testimony of support for the continuation of this level of
financial commitment or to our current medical beliefs. Albert
Einstein said many things, however, one of his most profound
observations says, “Insanity is doing the same thing over and
over and expecting a different outcome.”

This blind, and unjustified faith in the “medical system”
has cost us untold misery, early death, and unspeakable
amounts of money and in the end, our very way of life -
Americans are universally sick with cardiovascular disease,
obesity, diabetes, and arthritis; and the “American Dream”
(millions of our manufacturing, assembly and “IT” jobs have
left the country because of the outrageous cost of health care
benefits for employees) of owning your own home, now
seems beyond the average persons grasp - many of those who
did get variable mortgages and subprime loans during the
housing boom of the early 21st century have become perfect
victims in a “perfect storm.”

Immortality vii

It is ironic that the people of Andora, a small western
European country, are the longest lived people of the
industrialized world in 2008 and that some third world
countries have as many as 1:500 centenarians without access
to healthcare, while we Americans and other high-tech
industrialized nations with health care programs running out
of our ears, have only 1:5,000-10,000 centenarians - why do
they have up to 20 times the number of centenarians that we
do, after all we are dedicated to following the instructions of
our highly trained primary care physicians and specialists,
and the use of pharmaceuticals and surgery - aren’t we!

On the surface and to the casual observer, the countries,
cultures and subcultures with a high average life span and
high rates of centenarians seem vastly different from one
another - what on earth can the Okinawans have in common
with the Sardinians, Yao people, Hunzas, Georgians, Symiots,
Campodimelani, Costa Ricans, Vilcabamba, Titicaca, Seventh
Day Adventists and Mormons?

The secret of becoming a centenarian is “hidden” in
plain view! The epiphany revealed in IMMORTALITY, is a
compilation of the life’s work (94 years between the two of us)
of two physicians from separate worlds - China and America.

Between us we have traveled and studied the cultures,
diets, health practices, diseases (20,000 autopsies - 17,500 on
more than 400 species of captive wild zoo species and 3,000
humans) and the daily lives of the Chinese, Americans (all 50
states and the territories of Guam, the American Virgin Islands
and Puerto Rico), Canadians, Australians, New Zealanders,
Japanese, Okinawa, Mexico, Peru, Italy, Greece, South Africa,
East Africa, Central Africa, Israelis, England, Switzerland,
Denmark, Holland, Germany, Eastern Europe and the Balkan
states.

In IMMORTALITY, the two of us view health, life,
longevity, diseaseand deaththroughtheeyesandunderstanding
ofa farmer, animal and human nutrition, soil and plant nutrition
and chemistry, veterinarian, primary care physician, surgeon,

Chinese Traditional Medicine and pathologist - this is the first
time in history and human endeavor that these crafts, trades
and professions have come together under one roof to solve the
mysteries of the “centenarian club” and uncover the universal
currency of the age beaters.

Although markedly different in culture, tradition, diet
and dress, every human society from the simple primitive
band of a few dozen members to the great populations of
millions in large city states and vast countries share a common
thread that predictably, as predictable as gravity, produces
age beaters and centenarians - in effect IMMORTALITY!

This humble stuff, required by all who would become
a centenarian, was first employed by humans in a conscious
proactive way between 50,000 and 200,000 years ago. The use
of this byproduct of daily living changed the paths of human
culture so profoundly, so dramatically, that Jared Diamond, a
professor at the University of California describes this period
as “The Great Leap Forward.”

What makes “The Great Leap Forward” interesting is
that Professor Diamond, himself, never identified the stuff
that created the “Great Leap” - he theorized that the Great
Leap was produced by a perfection of the vocal cords “that
resulted in the ability to communicate better and pass on
information to the next generation.”

The universal stuff that brought humans the ability to
fulfill their genetic potential for living to be 100 years of age
was not that glamorous - no, it wasn’t a new and improved
larynx, nor was it the steam engine or electricity - it was the
humble plant minerals that were derived the ashes of carbon
fuel (wood, peat, rice straw, kelp, manure, etc.).

Ashes from carbon fuels, in their purist form are not
carbon, but rather they are a snow white collection of minerals
that the plant sucked up from the soil and sea water and were
the minerals that were left behind as heating and cooking fires
consumed the stored carbon or “solar currency” in the fuel.

Technology, starting with the Industrial Revolution,

Immortality ix

 

commercial use of fossil fuels, steam power and eventually the
universal employment of electricity, natural gas and propane
has been on one hand a plus for the greater human good and
the advancement of technology. However - there are always
unintended consequences!

Niether during the switch from wood as the universal
fuel to electricity, nor after words did anyone (doctor or
politician) say, “we no longer have easy or unconscious access
to traditional sources of plant minerals required for healthy
human and animal life and as a result we must supplement
with all of the known essential minerals.”

Asaresult of this historical failing to warn us, we moved
almost instantly from our historic and deep relationship with
the Earth and plant ashes (plant minerals) as a rich source of
nutritional minerals to a nutritional mineral vacuum.

In the case of health and longevity, we Americans have
been thrown under the bus by medical dogma (i.e.- “you can get
everything you need just by eating the four food groups” and
following “the principals of the seven food group pyramid”).

The epiphany is that today’s apple, bread and vegetable
are not the same as your grandfather’s apple, bread and
vegetable! Yes, in our industrialized world we produce more
tons and bushels of food than ever before, we feed billions of
people around the world with empty calories, however, a basic
ingredient in the modern process of farming, food production
and preparation - minerals - are no longer available in either
the proper spectrum or in optimal quantities - they are not
a self renewing resource and are today universally missing
from the human diet in the industrialized world!

The missing ingredient, plant minerals, was at one time
in history a universal and basic by-product of daily life. We
burned wood and other carbon fuels for heat, cooking and
light. We know specifically, that at 3:00 p.m. in the afternoon
on Monday September 4, 1882, that hundreds of thousands
of years of basking in and improving in the benefits of “The
Great Leap Forward” slowed, stalled and came to an end as

the source of our “Great Leap” dried up.

This moment in September 1882, is the time and date
on which Thomas Edison and his chief electrical technician,

first commercial electric generating plant. It was located
on Pearl Street, on a bluff, in New York City overlooking
the construction of the Brooklyn Bridge - wood, peat, kelp,
straw, manure, etc., the historic universal sources of fuel, our
historical source of the essential plant minerals for garden
fertilizer and human and animal nutrition (culinary ashes)
from the beginning of time, in the form of wood and plant ash
for all humans was now on the path to oblivion - the period
we've come to call “The Great Slide Backwards” began.

According to Dan Buettner, author of The Blue Zone,
demographer S. Jay Olshansky, University of Illinois at
Chicago, and his band of “50 of the world’s top longevity
experts” released a position paper in 2002 to be “as blunt as
they could fashion it...to squash charlatans forever.”

' The self-anointed Olshansky group stated, “Our
language on this matter must be unambiguous. There are no
lifestyle changes, surgical procedures, vitamins, antioxidants,
hormones, or techniques of genetic engineering available
today that have been demonstrated to influence the processes
of aging.”

If you take the Olshansky position paper literally, they,
were quite correct, however, they left out Calorie Restricted diets
and plant minerals. You cannot effectively use any nutrients,
phytonutrients or raw materials physiologically without
mineral cofactors - even oxygen and water are unavailable to
you at the cellular level without mineral cofactors!

The people found in most of the “Blue Zones” of
the world live in remote and poor environments, they
unconsciously subsist on environmentally imposed Calorie
Restricted diets of about 1,200 to 1,500 calories per day,

Wood ashes, peat moss ash, rice straw ash, kelp ash
and animal manure ash were the universal historic sources
of fertilizer and nutritional plant minerals for humans and

In countries where cultural cooking with wood, peat,

and the daily consumption of serious levels of antioxidants
still prevails, the greater concentrations and numbers of

To maximize your chances of reaching the status of

Chapter 1

“In a sense the human species is immortal. We as
individuals are not. We age and die. But some of the cells
in our body are part of a stream of cells we call the germ
line, or the reproductive lineage itself, and they have been

know what death is.”

Dr. Michael West, 2008
Biology visionary

Aging

In 1914, there was enough knowledge regarding the
need for vitamins and minerals for optimal health, maximum
productionand maximum longevity, that dozens of nutritional
companies sprang up that produced nutrient enriched feeds
for livestock. One such company was Purina Mills of St. Louis,
MO.

The use of vitamins and minerals in livestock feeds
became commonplace to increase survival, fertility, production
(more babies, meat, milk and eggs per pound of feed) and to
prevent and reverse diseases. Vitamins, minerals, amino acids
and essential fatty acids were added as a complete package
or “pre-mix” to pellets comprised of alfalfa, corn, soybeans,
wheat, oats and beet pulp.


 

To improve the efficiency of the enriched feeds and to
prevent the animals from sorting through the feed mix and
eating only what they preferred, the ration, the mix of grain,
hay, beans and vitamin-mineral premix was compressed
into uniform sized and nutritionally homogenous pellets.
Today, there are pelletized rations for laboratory animals of
all species, for pet animals of all species and for farm animals
of every species.

Over the years, this pelletized ration system was
perfected and became so universally successful, that their
use resulted in the elimination of (the prevention and cure of)
as many as 900 different diseases in animals that still plague
humans in 2008, and these dietary formulas have doubled and
quadrupled the life spans of all animal species in the home,
the farm, the laboratory and the zoological park!

This miraculous health and longevity benefit was
not due to the use of wonder drugs, genetically engineered
proteins, stem cells or organ transplants - these health and
longevity benefits were simply the result of the application
of nutritional science - complete nutrition in a pellet - what
a concept! Agricultural scientists were not going to let the
chicken’s food choices or cow’s food choices affect their own
health or the farmer’s production or income.

Sixty years ago, an “old dog” was ten years old, today an
“old dog” is 25 years of age; 100 years ago an old rat was three
to four years old, today an old rat is five to six years of age.
These gerontological miracles of more than doubling the life
spans of dogs, rats and other species was not engineered with
good organically grown food, exercise, genetically engineered
proteins, stem cell injections, pharmaceuticals, organ
transplants or bionic implants - this gerontological miracle
of eliminating disease and more than doubling life spans
in animals was brought about by simply adding vitamins,
minerals, trace minerals, rare earths, essential fatty acids,
amino acids and antioxidants to a commercially prepared diet
of alfalfa pellets or dog food - simply described as “lab chow”

===== Screenshot (15) =====

 

or “dog food.”
Dog food and lab animal chow formulations, for the

cost of only a few billion dollars over a period of 75 years,
solved the longev ity riddle in two ways:

1) providing all known essential nutrients in every
mouthful consumed, at every meal, every day.

2) providing all known essential nutrients at levels

sufficient to eliminate or significantly reduce the risks
of disabling, chronic, degenerative diseases and fatal
birth defects.

Since 1964, we have been on a quest to translate this
longevity miracle produced by nutrient enhanced “lab chow”
i dog food,” into an identical plan for humans.

After 44 years of implementation of our beliefs with
human patients, we have become quite adeptat accomplishing
this science-based goal in practical every day life situations
for literally millions of people every day.

Gilgamesh

The oldest known story relating to the active search for
the secrets of longevity is the 5,000 year old saga of Gilgamesh,
the Sumarian King from Uruk, who along with Enkidu, a good
friend and the general of his armies, decided they would live
forever.

When Enkidu, the king’s friend and general died
suddenly of disease in the mid-years of his life, Gilgamesh
panicke d and decided to put all of his fortune and energies
‘do or die search for eternal youth and immortality.”
Gilgamesh climbed to the tops of the highest mountains,

into a’


 

searched the dense forests and dove deep into the depths of
rivers and oceans in his legendary, frantic and fanatical search
for youth, longevity and immortality.

Gilgamesh was originally regarded as a gerontological
myth until the discovery of 12 ancient clay tablets that
archeologists determined to be 5,000 years old. Pieced together
after their discovery in the ancient city of Sumar, the tablets
confirmed that Gilgamesh was in fact a king, adventurer
and hero of his times who led a great quest for the “key to
immortality” - he called for a “revolt against death itself!!”

As the legend goes, Gilgamesh eventually and
heroically found a wise old man in the high mountains, who
spoke of a fragrant flower that “looked like a buckthorn and
prickled like a rose.”

The wise prophet shared with Gilgamesh that the plant
could be found at the floor of the world’s deepest sea, and by
coming into possession of this plant and tasting it, one could
regain their youth.

Gilgamesh located the life preserving and longevity
promoting plant, however, before he could eat sufficient
quantities of it to rejuvenate himself, it was stolen from him
by a serpent seeking a new skin.

Was this anti-aging plant a form of kelp or sea vegetable
that contained significant amounts of plant minerals and
antioxidants?

Chronological Age

Certainly each of us appreciates the reality of
chronological age, the period of existence of a person or age
arranged according to irreversible sequences of time.

Chronological aging is as predictable as gravity. When
you are 75 years old you are in fact 75 years old. There are 365

Cosmetics, face lifts, derm-abrasion, Botox injections,
breast enhancement, tummy-tucks and liposuction can make
people look ten to 25 years younger, you will feel better about
yourself, however, none of these procedures will add ten
years of chronological time to your life, help you attain your
genetic potential for longevity, prevent or cure chronic and
degenerative diseases - essentially, cosmetics and “plastic
procedures alone, without a complete fitness and
nutrition program, are the equivalent of putting a classy new
paint job on a 50 year old rusted out and poorly maintained
worn out car - why not have it all, why not do both - go for it,
do the non-toxic and non-invassive beauty practices and the
nutrition program?

Biological Aging

Biological aging, when compared to chronological
is another matter. Biological aging, uninterrupted,
is a progressive time-related breakdown in an organism's
ability to respond, alter and adapt to its internal and external

aging

environment.

The result of uninterrupted biological aging is as
predictable as gravity - you will die long before your genetic
capacity for longevity and that your last months and years
will be miserable, painful and in industrialized societies very,
very costly!

Just having “good genes” and a grandmother who
lived to be 103 years of age is not enough - it only tells you
that you have the possibility of getting there - but only if you
do everything right!

Theories of Biological Aging

There are two basic theories that attempt to explain the
process of biological aging - 1) genetic and 2) stochastic.

Genetic Theory

All human beings have the genetic equipment and
therefore the capacity to live well past 100 years of age - it
doesn’t matter if everyone in your family died at age 35 years
of age, you, as an individual, with conscious effort, can still
live beyond 100 years of age.

Conversely, if all your relatives lived to be older than
110 years of age, you are not quaranteed to get there yourself,
unless YOU do everything right!

In general, genetics give no guarantee or warrantee that
one will live to be 100 years of age, run a hundred meters in

The “Stochastic” Nature of Aging

Gerontologists and doctors, in their
pontificate and perpetuate the dogma that there is nothing that
you can do to ensure that you will reach the centenarian club.
They preach endlessly that there are no common threads that
can be used by all, to successfully reach 100 - they preach that
aging is “stochastic” or uncontrollable or a gamble that only a
few lucky ones from the “Blue Zones” will randomly attain.

We know for an irrefutable fact that aging or longevity
has a genetic upper limit or genetic potential in all species,
including man, yet, our input, either conscious or unconscious
is required to consistently achieve or reach the upper limit of
our genetic potential for longevity.

Having “good genes” alone will certainly not get you
to 100 years of age in a healthful way without you doing
everything else right! Just having “good genes” is not a

A plethora of factors, including nutritional competency
and deficiencies, affect the course of events in an individual's

to reach their maximum potential for longevity.

Aging is not an all or nothing process - aging is like a
puzzle, a few pieces cannot give you the full and complete picture,
as the pieces of the puzzle are put into the structure of the puzzle,
the greater the ability to see the complete and full picture.

Cellular Immortality

In 1912, Dr. Alexis Carrel, who had won the Nobel
Prize for developing the original technique for “end to end
anastomosis (joining) of blood vessels with fine suture,”
began to grow undifferentiated fibroblasts (connective tissue
cells aka “stem cells”) from chicken hearts in flasks in his
laboratory at the Rockefeller Institute.

He fed these isolated and undifferentiated fibroblast or
“stem cells” with filtered extracts of blended chicken embryos
and the “stem cells” kept multiplying and growing for 34
years (including 2 years after Carrel’s own death).

These early “cell cultures” were so successful that,
excessive numbers were shared with other investigators or
discarded “to prevent them from performing one of those 2n
multiplication feats (“Andromatous Strain”) and cover the
earth.”

Carrel’s work led to the theory that, “Cells are
inherently immortal if given an ideal environment, and the
aging of organisms we see in the laboratory and in the mirror

===== Screenshot (20) =====

must be due to interactions between cells, or to some central
mechanism, hormonal perhaps, whose influence reaches
everywhere.”

~ Two years after his death, Carrel’s colleagues got bored
with the experiment and threw the 34 year old fibroblast
cultures out. However, Carrel’s experiment resulted in the
theory that “cells are inherently immortal if they are fed a
perfect diet and kept in a perfect environment.”

In 1961, Dr. Leonard Hayflick and Paul Moorhead
at the Wistar Institute in Philadelphia challenged Carrel’s
findings. Hayflick, currently at the School of Medicine at the
University of California, San Francisco, is a highly regarded
biogerontologist.

In his experiment, Hayflick, using his own growth
media, human embryonic lung fibroblasts could only “ double”
50 times with a predictable intracellular accumulation of
ceroid lipofuscin (pigments of aging that indicate a selenium
deficiency) that eventually disabled and killed the cells.

Hayflick’s definition of “doubling” was that the culture
flask became filled with new cells and he would dump 50% of
the cells to make living space for continuation of the cultures.
How many, individual duplications, of Carrel’s chicken
embryo heart fibroblast cells, did it take to fill the flask with
the new cells - hundreds, thousands, millions?

Fibroblasts begin looking old as they near the
“doubling” limit. When viewed under the microscope they
become larger and accumulate an increased quantity of the
golden “pigment of aging” - ceroid lipofuscin.

Hayflick attributed Carrel’s chicken heart fibroblast
longevity success to “contamination” by new embryonic
fibroblasts from the chick embryo media.

Hayflick’s 50 “doubling” limit in normal human lung
cells has come to be known as the “Hayflick limit.”

When old nuclei are inserted into young cells and
young nuclei are inserted into old cells, the data indicates that
the physiological age of the cell, the number of “doublings”


 

they have left, is controlled by the nucleus. It suggests that
there is a programmed synthesis of a “senescence factor” in
old cells and that this factor inhibits DNA replication when
the music stops - suggesting that the “ Hayflick limit” may in
fact may be alterable and is not set in concrete!

Using the same growth media, the Hayflick limit
varies from species to species and appears to be related to the
maximum known life span of the species.

Hayflick Limit by Species

 

Species Hayflick Limit

Hayflick learned that when he froze his cell culture after
20 doublings, they could “remember” that they hada maximum
of 30 doublings left when they were thawed and re-fed.

HeLa cells were the first human cells to be successfully
propagated outside the body in significant numbers. HeLa
cells, which came from a biopsy of a cervical cancer, “ignore
the Hayflick limit for human cells. In a sense they are
immortal.” They will continue to grow and divide as long as
they have nutrients, oxygen, space and means of getting rid of
their waste.

The human donor for the original HeLa cells was
Henrietta Lacks, a poor black woman from Baltimore, whose
identity was only revealed years after her cells were being
proliferated around the world and long after she had died.

Regulating Life Span

Regulating Life Span

Numerous methods have been developed for
estimating physiological age. The most commonly employed
measure, especially in population studies, is blood pressure
levels, blood sugar levels and age at death. Statistical analysis
of mortality data can be employed to separate age-related
deaths (chronic disease and sudden death) from age-unrelated
deaths (car accidents and house fires).

Any complete understanding of aging will necessarily
and eventually explain conditions and treatments that increase
the maximum life span. At least three methods of increasing
maximum life span in laboratory animals (that are fed perfect
diets) are known:

1) lower metabolic rate (hypothermia)
2) Calorie Restricted intake (without malnutrition)

3) enhance antioxidant protection (genetic engineering
and supplements)

Lowering Metabolic Rate

Decreasing the rate of metabolism either by lowering
the ambient temperature of cold-blooded animals or by
restricting movement, can significantly extend the life spans
of many invertebrate species. These facts point to the fact that
the rate of metabolic output can affect longevity.


 

The “old ones” (those over age 75) in all Blue Zones
spend a lot of time sunning themselves, gleaning large doses
of vitamin D, from the sun (by sitting in the sun or gardening)
and lowering their metabolic rate each day by consciously
devoting time to “siesta time” or meditation.

Sohal reported that 200 houseflies maintained in a one
cubic foot cage (to stimulate flight activity) resulted ina mean
lifespan of 14 days.

Housing smaller numbers of houseflies in a large cage
resulted in an average life span of about 35 days.

Placing the flies in small bottles with a cardboard
maze, that permitted them to walk, but eliminated their flight
activity, extended mean life span to 70 days.

Further decreasing the metabolic activity of the flies by
placing them in small vials at a decreased temperature (18
degrees C) extended their life span to 120 days.

The total amount of oxygen consumed during life by
each group of flies (per unit weight) was identical.

The maximum survival rate or longevity for fish has
been extended by 300% in the UCLA laboratory of Dr. Roy
Walford by maximizing the micronutrients (minerals) fed to
the fish as well as lowering the water temperature by just a
few degrees.

Within individual species, the metabolic rate of
individuals clearly affects life span. Dr. Thomas Perls has
produced the clearest description of this relationship in “The
Rate of Living Theory.”

According to Perls’ hypothesis, all species exhibit
a genetically determined metabolic potential and species’
specific life span is therefore dependent on the rate of
metabolism. Many studies have confirmed the validity of this
observation in cold-blooded animals.

Calorie Restriction without Malnutrition

Gerontologists theorize that our prehistoric ancestors
clung to life through long periods of time without food - the
human body’s amazing survival mechanisms would then
come into play - slow metabolism and slow aging. This
mechanism allowed humans to survive through times of
drought and famine.

The profile of people who actively pursued longevity
is found in the writings of authors on longevity who, living
by their beliefs, lived to at least their tenth decade. Five of the
most famous of this group are Luigi Cornaro, Sir Hermann
Weber, Sir James Crichton-Browne, Dr. Alexandre Gueniot,
and Scott Nearing.

The most well known and famous of these five longevity

icons is Luigi Cornaro. Luigi Cornaro, wrote The Art of Living
Long, one of the four most famous autobiographies of the
Renaissance (the piece was translated into every European
language and later endorsed by George Washington). Cornaro
was born in Venice in 1464 and died in Padua at the age of 103
years in 1567.
Cornaro was born into Italian nobility, he practiced “a
dissolute, gluttonous life,” which lead to a disabling ill-health
by the age of 37. Cornaro was morbidly obese, suffered from
gout, “stomach fever,” cold temperatures (hypothyroidism)
and generalized pain (arthritis, fibromyalgia, etc.).

Cornaro’s physicians gave him little hope for survival
for one year so he decided to become his own physician; he
asked the old women working in the fields what they ate.

After these interviews, Cornaro became aware of the
peasant’s Calorie Restricted diet (imposed by access only to
small plots of land and heavy taxation) and became convinced
that his maladies, as with most diseases of the human species,
were due to a life of intemperance, homage to the “belly gods”
and the “nervous” life they commanded.


 

Cornaro consciously changed course and “he
voluntarily practiced a severe, rigid, temperate and dietarily
restrictive routine.”

Cornaro’s diet was the same every day and some meals
consisted of a single egg yolk. He faithfully ate “12 ounces of
solids, a little meat, one egg yolk, a slice of bread, vegetable
soup and 14 ounces of young wine,” which provided a total
of 1,000 calories or less” per day.

The part that wine, “the milk of the old,” played
in Cornaro’s diet is considered a historical “fluke” by
gerontologists. Cornaro liked young local wines. '

Cornaro faithfully practiced this Calorie Restricted
dietary routine for the rest of his life (he never retired, rather
he worked his estate from horseback, counseled members
of the Paduan society as a revered elder and wrote books
on health until the end) - he became lean and his health
prospered. Despite his sinful younger years, Cornaro lived
to be 103 years of age and reported in his autobiography that,
“he was high for his remaining sixty-six years.”

Four hundred years after the time of Cornaro, three
European physicians of some great fame made longevity
one of their main interests. Each of the three doctors had
many long-lived patients; all three wrote books or essays on
longevity when they were in their 90s.

Sir Hermann Weber (1823-1918) stressed exercise as well
as a Calorie Restricted lactovegetarian diet - he recommended
one to three hours of walking each day and vacations that
included climbing, hiking and hunting.

Sir James Crichton-Browne (1840-1938) commented on
Cardinal de Satis, who lived to be 110, cited a mix of a Calorie
Restricted diet, walking and riding daily and to “continuing
projects throughout one’s life.”

Dr. Alexandre Guenoit (1832-1935) of Paris, and
president of the Paris Medical Academy, followed a somewhat
less restrictive Cornaro-type diet as a lactovegetarian, walked
up 56 steps to his apartment even until age 99 years to gather

===== Screenshot (24) =====

his notes, write his book and died in 1935 at the age of 102
years.

Today, it is a well established fact that consuming fewer
calories (Calorie Restricted diets) can and does extend life.

Scott Nearing (1883-1983), like Cornaro became a
legend in his own lifetime. Alone or with his wife Helen,
Nearing wrote on the subjects of pacifism, socialism and
lactovegetarian beliefs.

In 1932, the Nearings bought a farm in Vermont and
began to live outScott’s alternative culture beliefs. They farmed
with hand tools and implements for more than 50 years. Their
work included digging, hoeing, weeding, composting (wood
ashes) and hauling. In addition, Scott did his own sawing
and chopping of wood for their wood-burning stove. They
have constructed 15 stone buildings on their two homesteads.
Scott calls them “one-man/one-woman” projects, and Helen
admits that, “she became so involved in the (construction of
the) Maine house, that she personally selected and placed
every stone.” That home was finished in 1977, when Scott was
94 years of age and Helen was 73.

The Nearings rarely if ever used doctors, pills, or
hospitals, yet Scott lived to a “hale and hearty 100 and died
when he decided to by fasting for a month and a half at the
very end.”

~ Nearing had always been physically active, in the
woods, in the garden, and in building construction. He was
also active mentally, having written 40 books, including an
autobiography - The Making of a Radical.

A month or two before he died, Scott was sitting at the
dinner table and said, “I think I won’t eat anymore.”

On his last day, Helen and Scott were together at home;
no interruptions; no doctors or hospitals. They were quiet
together and Helen said, “It’s alright Scott. Go right along.
You've lived a good life and are finished with things here. We
love you and let you go. It’s alright.”

In 1935, Clive McKay, a professor of physiology at


 

Cornell University observed that a Calorie Restricted diet,
or “under nutrition without malnutrition” could extend life.
With a 60% reduction of free choice calories at weaning age,
came a doubling of the 50% survival rate and doubled the
known maximum life span of laboratory rats!

The McKay Calorie Restricted diet was super-supplemented with extra vitamins and minerals. On this 60%
regimen, the animal’s growth rate was significantly reduced,
however, in all other ways they were super healthy.

The CR diet fed rats could be held in this growth
retarded condition for up to 1,000 days, by which time all
of the traditionally fed rats had died. When the CR diet rats
were allowed a full diet, growth started again. The rats were
also more sexually active and reproduced normally at a more
advanced age than the rats fed the traditional diet.

In the 1960s and 1970s, Morris Ross of the Institute
for Cancer Research in Philadelphia, conducted a series of
experiments that produced a maximum life span of 1,000 to
1,099 days in fully fed rats and 1,600 to 1,699 days in CR fed
rats - a 60% increase. These studies pitted the standard lab
diets for rats against the reduced amounts of the enriched
diets - the classic “under nutrition without malnutrition.”

These experiments have been repeated successfully
many times in many species of animals. In fact, a Calorie
Restricted (CR) diet “without malnutrition” is the only
intervention that has ever consistently, predictably and
successfully increased both the mean and maximal life span
of mammals.

The positive effects of Calorie Restriction without
malnutrition have been observed in diverse vertebrate and
invertebrate species. Diet-restricted mammals retain youthful
characteristics, including immune function, vitality, long after
their higher calorie fed litter mates are dead.

Calorie Restricted diets have been studied in the rhesus
monkey by gerontologist Richard Weindruch, at the University

of Wisconsin at Madison. Only four of his CR monkeys have

===== Screenshot (25) =====

died, half the death rate of the standard fed monkeys. The
CR monkeys have not developed diabetes, w hile 50% of the
standard diet monkeys have developed the disease.

Because most investigators of CR diets forget the

“without malnutrition” piece it is not surprising that the lab
animals on unsupplemented CR diets develop diseases of
malnutrition including a significant drop in bone density,
reduced libido, dysmenorr hea, infertility and wide fluctuations
in body temperature.

Calorie Restricted diets consistently produce biological
effects that are directly linked, in laboratory animals, to longer
life. Understanding the mechanisms of these biological effects
helps to translate theory into practical application.

Some of the obvious CR effects were quickly identified
in the early studies, additional long term effects have been
identified as the studies have continued; we also realize that we
can enhance or modify these CR effects by conscious choices:

1) lowers circulating glucose levels

2) lowers circulating insulin levels

3) lowers percentage of body fat

4) lowers blood & tissue levels of growth stimulators
5) reduces cell loss from the aging process

6) decreases body load of “inflammation” or “free radical

injury.”

7) encourages a youthful physiology Under nutrition
(Caloric Restricted diet) without malnutrition (diet
contains elevated levels of vitamins and minerals
above the RDA) experiments are most effective in
laboratory animals when started in infancy, however,


 

they also show dramatic longevity benefit even when
the low calorie/nutrient dense diet is begun in mid-adulthood.

In Luigi Cornaro’s example, he intervened at age 37 and
lived to be 103. This observation should give all individuals
encouragement to change their habits for the better and
intensely supplement - it’s never too late!

Low calorie/nutrient dense diets may not be for all
human children as there is a relatively high mortality rate on
the front end of this type of program. Children can flourish and
do well on optimal caloric intake/nutrient dense nutritional
programs.

In the 1940s, professors A.H. Carlson and F. Holzol
added a creative twist to the concept of the CR diet by studying
the effects of intermittent periods of fasting on the life span
of rats. They free choice fed rats a high-quality diet rich in
vitamins and minerals in unlimited amounts, but fasted them
completely every other day; another group was fasted every
third day and another group fasted every fourth day.

The maximum life span in the un-fasted animals was
800 days, however, in all three intermittently fasted groups
the maximum life span was extended to 1,000 to 1,100 days-a
20 to 30% increase in maximum life span.

Dr. Charles Goodrick of the National Institute on
Aging (Gerontology Research Unit) studied intermittent
fasting by feeding the control a perfect diet while the study
group was fasted every other day. Maximum life span was
increased from the 875 days of the normally fed rats to 1,295
in the restricted rats, from 630 days 50% survival to 980 days.

5% Calorie Reduction

A drop of as little as 5% of your typical daily calorie
intake will certainly improve your health.

At this level of caloric reduction, a woman will reduce
her daily calorie total by 100 calories, w hich means give up
the slice of toast for breakfast or only eat one half of that
hamburger bun for lunch; a man will give up 125 calories ata
5% calorie reduction.

A 5% calorie reduction could be as simple as giving
up the 3:00 pm two scoop ice cream snack for a one scoop
portion.

10% to 15% Calorie Reduction

A reduction of 10% to 15% of your typical caloric
intake will trigger a metabolic event, known as “hormesis.”
Hormesis refers to limiting your caloric intake in an intelligent
manner, so that you challenge your body with a beneficial
physiological or met tabolic stress or “hormesis.”

With fewer easy fuel calories available for
production, your body shifts from the command to store fat
to the command to preferentially burn fat as fuel for energy

energy

production.

Eventually (usually within a week) your body becomes
more efficient in its ability to produce energy from fat and
your stamina improves. The need for an increased rate of cell
reproduction and replacement i is reduced, allowing the body
to direct more effort towards defenses against free radical
damage and extended life!

To maximize the positive CR effect, reduce your daily
protein intake to between 45 to 60 grams for men and 40 to 50


 

grams for women.

A 10% caloric reduction for women translates to
reducing the total daily caloric intake by 200 and 250 for men.
A 15% reduction would translate to a reduced daily caloric
intake for women by 300 and 475 for men.

In practical terms, this means giving up the daily ration
of potatoes and eating more green vegetables; give up the
pancakes or waffles for breakfast and eat a veggie omelette.

20% Calorie Reduction

A 20% reduction in daily caloric intake will translate to
women giving up 400 calories and men giving up 500 calories.

Giving up 20% of your daily calories will translate
to giving up Hive and replacing it with an 80 to 100 calorie
protein shake, an egg or our favorite, a pre-bagged handful of
mixed salted nuts.

It is not unusual for a slight hunger awareness to creep
into your life at this point. The way to eliminate the hunger
sensation is to increase your daily dose of certified organic
plant minerals and have a small snack consisting of one
handful (remember, its only one) of mixed salted nuts.

Fat vs Thin

Do naturally thin or skinny people live longer than those
individuals of “normal” weight? To answer this question, a
large study of mortality versus body weight was conducted
among 750,000 men and women by Drs. Lew and Garfinkel of

the American Cancer Society.

The overall mortality of people who were more than

===== Screenshot (28) =====

 

10% lighter than average, was actually increased - an apparent
paradox. McKay found that the degree of body fat in later life
was more important than such variables as percent protein
intake or even exercise. Overtly fat rats lived shorter lives.

It is of interest to note here that Sir Winston Churchill
was obese, spent most of his adult life in bed until noon,
smoked a whole box of Cuban cigars daily and drank a quart
of brandy per day and died at 89 years of age - being thin by
itself is not the “secret ingredient” for a long healthy life.

McKay’s rats that were unable to gain weight and fatten
on a totally permissive diet, and tended, despite the diet, to
remain at a weight less than average, also lived considerably
shorter lives. Therefore, being “ naturally thin” and unable to
gain weight in spite of excessive food intake is not the same
health-wise as thinness induced by dietary restriction.

People, who remain underweight, despite increased
food intake, display a slightly increased metabolic rate and
increased body temperature, both of which reduce maximum
life span.

If you are the type of individual who gains weight
easily by overeating, but you elect to lose weight by CR diets,
then you have an increased survival advantage!

Naturally thin individuals can live longer by becoming
even thinner, so long as there is no malnutrition - you too
must get into the CR lifestyle and ramp up on the your intake
of essential nutrients (plant minerals) and antioxidants!

Numerous biochemical measurements show that CR

animals are considerably bio-chemically younger than their

chronological age.


 

Antioxidant Defenses

A third laboratory method used to increase life span is
to genetically engineer animals to over express genes tied to
oxidant or free radical removal. Oxygen free radicals and other
reactive oxygen species (ROS) are thought to be important
factors in a large number of disease processes and aging.

Because of the very toxic affects of ROS, aerobic survival
depends on the prevention of the associated oxidative impact.
Antioxidant enzyme systems are normally present in all
healthy aerobic cells - remember, antioxidants also need raw
materials and mineral cofactors to operate at their optimum
capacity (glutathione requires selenium, etc.).

The superoxide dismutases (SOD) quench or neutralize
superoxide radicals (O”,) but create H,O, as a byproduct. Cells
also have high levels of non-enzymatic, low molecular weight
antioxidants. These non-enzymatic anti-oxidants remove the
potent hydroxyl radical (-HO:), a very potent byproduct of
the O, and H,0O, free radical reactions.

Fruit flies genetically engineered to over express
only SOD or catalase show little or no change in longevity.
However, when the fruit flies are genetically engineered to
over express both SOD and catalase, they are able to increase
the mean and maximum life span by as much as 40%.

These studies suggest that the quenching of O, and
H,O, free radicals can positively affect the basic processes
that control aging.

===== Screenshot (29) =====

Free Radical Theory of Aging

Free radicals are a diverse and destructive group of
agents which can be brought into the body transdermally and
by breathing, eating and drinking; they can also be produced
in significant numbers internally during normal metabolism
and as a byproduct of a fat-burning weight loss program.

These highly reactive substances differ from other less
harmful molecules in that they possess an extra electrical
charge - a free unpaired radical or a “ free” electron.

Free radicals set off abnormal reactions at the cellular
level that are typically “once-only,” irreversible, and
energetically wasteful.

Most free radicals that the human body is exposed to
are oxidants, “their free electron whirling around a super-activated oxygen atom (i.e. - hydroxyl free radical, singlet
oxygen, and hydrogen peroxide).

Dr. Roy Walford referred to free radicals as the “Great
white sharks in the biochemical sea” - these short-lived but
voracious chemicals oxidize and damage tissue, particularly
cell membranes and mitochondrial membranes.

Free radical “shark attacks” on polyunsaturated fats
and cholesterol found in tissue membranes, produce lipid
peroxides, which in turn decompose to yield chemicals known
as aldehydes, which cross-link proteins, lipids and DNA.

Walford described oxygen based free radicals very
well, “Not everybody realizes that the oxygen of our
atmosphere is a highly poisonous gas. Alien entities arriving
from a methane planet would choke and die on the stuff we
breathe so casually. It rusts iron, is involved in the decay and
crumbling of granite boulders, and participates in every fire
in town...yet we live in it (require it) and love The

Lipids become “peroxidized” or damaged as a result
of interactions with ROS or free radicals. This damage results


 

in structural, geometrical or “steric” physical changes that
decrease membrane fluidity.

Aging decreases membrane fluidity because of oxidant-induced depletion of unsaturated lipids. As a result, aging
membranes become more rigid (nutrition and oxygen can’t
go in and wastes can’t get out), significant negative changes
are then recorded in membrane-bound proteins. Membrane-bound enzymes and mitochondrial enzymes are regularly
found to display age-related changes in activity.

Peroxidized lipid molecules undergo several
rearrangements to form highly reactive cross-linking agents,
such as malondialdehyde (MDA). MDA reacts with amino
groups of proteins and amino acids, further increasing the
negative effects of free radical oxidation on cell wall lipids.

The end byproduct of MDA activity and damage is a
fluorescent pigment called ceroid lipofuscin. This pigment, a
product of oxidized cell wall and mitochondrial wall lipids
will accumulate in the cell cytoplasm itself over time.

Percent of Ceroid Lipofuscin in Aging Human Brain Cells

Age  % intracellular volume occupied

30 - 40  35
50 - 60  41
70 - 80  55
90 - 100  78

(Adelman, et al., 1988)

===== Screenshot (30) =====
 


The medical “dogma” goes like this: as these ceroid
lipofuscin pigments accumulate in the cell over time, they
interfere with the internal physiological functioning of the cell
and mitochondria. Medical doctors, and most gerontologists,
falsely believe the accumulated aging pigments are not
preventable, removable or reversible and as they reach
significant levels, the lipofuscin-gorged and disrupted cells
and mitochondria “age,” become dysfunctional, diseased and
die.

Ceroid lipofuscin shows up in humans as “age spots”
or “liver spots” on the skin of the hands or face. If you have
one liver spot or age spot on the outside you have millions of
them on the inside!

The number and intensity of pigment levels in age
spots are directly related to the level of free radical damage
and can be a predictive barometer of sinister things to come -
i.e. - cataracts, macular degeneration, coronary artery disease,
diabetes, senile dementia, Alzheimer’s disease, cancer, etc.

Fortunately, the accumulation of ceroid lipofuscin
in the brain, heart, liver, lungs, bone marrow, eyes, etc. can
easily be prevented and quickly reversed. Cell culture studies,
animal studies and human studies demonstrate very clearly
that the accumulation of ceroid lipofuscin, the “pigment of
aging,” is not set in concrete and in fact can be prevented and
reversed.

Preventing and reversing the accumulation of ceroid
lipofuscin pigments is well documented in many animal
species and humans - to accomplish this “antiaging” feat
requires a significant reduction of the intake of free radicals
(fried foods, trans fats, burnt fats, heterocyclic amines, super-heated carbohydrates, acrylamides, nitrites, alcohol, sugar,
etc.) and a significant increase in the intake of all known
essential nutrients and an extraordinary increased intake of
antioxidants (20,000 to 25,000 ORAC points/ day).

The obvious question is, if free radicals cause aging
and contribute to the infirmities of aging, then shouldn’t


 

a reduction of free radical damage slow the rate of aging
and extend longevity ever closer to the individual’s genetic
potential for longevity and even beyond the generally accepted
maximum life span for humans. Such studies have shown that
increases in antioxidant intakes alone will increase the mean
life span of a population but not the maximum life span.

Considerable evidence has been collected to support
the idea that the 50% survival rate of a population can be
extended significantly by the consumption of combinations
of antioxidants, including vitamins A, C, E, selenium, sulfur
bearing amino acids (methionine, cysteine, cystine, etc.) and
phytochemicals such as catechens (green tea, coffee, red wine,
dark berries, exotic fruit, etc.), resveratrol (red wine, red
grape juice, giant Japanese knotweed, etc.), proanthocyanins
(red grape skins and seeds), leutines (green leafy vegetables),
lycopenes (tomatoes), flavonols and polyphenols (dark
chocolate, red wine, green tea, etc.).

Aging is chronological and progressive and is
typically the normal course of events in all biological
systems, however, decreased metabolic rate, Caloric
Restriction (without malnutrition) and an increase in the
levels of antioxidant protection will all significantly increase
longevity.

To fulfill one’s genetic potential for longevity they
must consume large amounts of antioxidants, consciously
supplement daily with all known essential nutrients (with a
special focus on minerals) and eat a Calorie Restricted diet and
equally as important, they must avoid as many land mines as
possible.

Essential Nutrients

Essential nutrients are referred to as “essential
nutrients” for two reasons:

1) We as humans can’t manufacture certain nutrients
that are required for health, reproduction and optimal
longevity - in fact, because we can’t manufacture them,
we must consume them in optimal amounts each and
every day, every meal and every mouthful either as
food or as supplements.

2) Failing to consume the essential nutrients in optimal
amounts with every mouthful of food, with every
meal, every day, we will, with the predictability of
gravity, fall ill, become infertile, bear children with
birth defects and produce lives that are unnecessarily
short, expensive and miserable!

“endangered species ” in its natural environment.

I went on to enter and graduate from the School of

Marlin Perkins gave me high recommendations which

Perkins and Dr. Barry Commoner, a professor of Botany
at Washington University, had gotten a $7.5 million grant
from the National Institutes of Health, to perform autopsies
on zoo animals and urban dwelling humans who had died of
“natural causes” to determine whether or not pollution might
be a factor in their causes of death.

As a comparative pathologist, I (Wallach) performed
more than 20,000 autopsies of over 17,500 captive wild
animals, laboratory species and domestic animals, of over 454
species and 3,000 humans for a comparison.

These autopsies included the review of clinical records,
microscopic and electron microscopic examinations of
histopathology, microbiology culture investigations for fungi,
bacteria and viruses and chemical and biochemical profiles.

The original purpose of this huge multidisciplinary
project (funded by the NIH, multiple zoos, Yerke’s Regional
Primate Research Center and multiple universities), known
as The Center for the Biology of Natural Systems, was to
investigate the possibility of environmental pollution and food
contamination contributing to causes of illness and death.

What was found was that, “every animal and every
human being that dies of natural causes dies of a nutritional
deficiency disease!”

Diets were formulated, supplemented and corrected
for species that until then were considered “too sensitive
to reproduce in captivity.” Fertility levels, pregnancies and
reproduction records skyrocketed in species that had historically
displayed little or no inclination to reproduce in captivity.

The principles of nutrition that had become successful


 

for providing health, fertility and longevity in laboratory
species, pet animals and farm animals had now proven to be
appropriate and highly successful for a wide variety of captive
wild and exotic species.

To make a long story short, I (Wallach) went back
to school, became a naturopathic physician (a primary
care physician in many states) and began to use veterinary
nutritional formulas on my human patients in 1978. It was
very quickly evident that the “truths” and “nutritional laws”
learned from our nutritional studies in 454 species of animals
worked exactly the same for humans!

In fact the principles of nutrition are the same for all
species of animals and humans. We do in fact require 90
essential nutrients with each and every mouthful of food,
each meal and every day:

60 essential minerals
